WARP-RM: A Warp-Augmented Relative Progress Reward Model for Data Curation
Authors: Justin Yu, Andrew Goldberg, Kavish Kondap, Karim El-Refai, Ethan Ransing, Qianzhong Chen, Mac Schwager, Fred Shentu, Philipp Wu, Ken Goldberg
First: 2026-06-26T17:58:06+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-10T14:49:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Project1
Abstract
Scaling imitation learning requires large datasets, yet human teleoperation inevitably produces mixed-quality demonstrations containing hesitations and recoveries. Prior frame-level progress reward models supervise on absolute temporal progress proxies that suffer from label noise, or require costly human annotations to define subtask boundaries. We present WARP (Warp-Augmented Relative Progress), a novel fully self-supervised algorithm for learning dense, signed relative progress magnitudes directly from successful demonstrations. WARP generates per-frame progress targets via time-warp augmentations of demonstrations (variable playback speeds and reversals) and we train WARP-RM to predict the normalized elapsed time between input frames. Aggregating these predictions across overlapping windows yields a dense frame-level progress signal. We then introduce WARP-BC, which leverages these scalar reward estimates to upweight high-advantage action chunks during behavior cloning, where chunk-level advantage is obtained by aggregating per-frame rewards. We evaluate our approach on a physical bimanual robot system performing a long-horizon deformable object manipulation task: folding T-shirts from a random crumpled start. To evaluate policy robustness against suboptimal data, we construct training datasets of varying quality using episode length as a proxy for teleoperation sub-optimality. As the dataset is widened to admit more inefficiencies, WARP-BC maintains a 19/20 success rate compared to vanilla BC's collapse to 2/20, improving throughput by up to 18x. Furthermore, we evaluate a bottle-in-bin placement task in the real-world, as well as in a reproducible simulation of the task, where gains in success, speed, and throughput hold under paired significance tests, and we release all simulation code and evaluation artifacts. Project page: https://uynitsuj.github.io/warp-rm/
中文标题/摘要
标题：WARP-RM：一种基于时间扭曲增强的相对进度奖励模型，用于数据整理
扩展模仿学习需要大量数据集，但人类遥操作不可避免地会产生包含犹豫和恢复的混合质量演示。先前的帧级进度奖励模型依赖于绝对时间进度代理进行监督，但存在标签噪声问题，或需要昂贵的人工标注来定义子任务边界。我们提出了WARP（时间扭曲增强相对进度），一种全新的完全自监督算法，可直接从成功演示中学习密集的、带符号的相对进度幅度。WARP通过演示的时间扭曲增强（可变播放速度和倒放）生成每帧的进度目标，并训练WARP-RM预测输入帧之间的归一化经过时间。在重叠窗口上聚合这些预测，可得到密集的帧级进度信号。随后我们引入WARP-BC，利用这些标量奖励估计，在行为克隆中提升高优势动作块的权重，其中块级优势通过聚合每帧奖励获得。我们在执行长时域可变形物体操作任务（从随机褶皱状态折叠T恤）的物理双臂机器人系统上评估该方法：随着训练数据集质量下降（以任务回合长度作为遥操作次优性的代理），WARP-BC保持19/20的成功率，而普通BC则崩溃至2/20，吞吐量提升最高达18倍。此外，我们在真实世界和可复现的仿真环境中评估了瓶装物品放入箱中的任务，成功、速度和吞吐量的提升在配对显著性检验中均成立，我们同时公开了所有仿真代码和评估材料。项目页面：https://uynitsuj.github.io/warp-rm/
Summary / 总结
Motivated by the challenge of scaling imitation learning with noisy human demonstrations, this work introduces WARP-RM, a self-supervised method that learns dense frame-level progress signals from successful demonstrations without human annotations. It generates per-frame progress targets by applying time-warp augmentations (speed variations and reversals) to demonstrations and trains a model to predict normalized elapsed time between frames. These predictions are aggregated across overlapping windows to form a continuous progress signal, which is then used in WARP-BC to prioritize high-advantage action chunks during behavior cloning. Evaluated on a physical robot folding T-shirts and a bottle-in-bin task, WARP-BC outperforms vanilla behavior cloning, maintaining 19/20 success rate versus 2/20 as dataset quality degrades, and achieves up to 18x higher throughput.
为了解决人类演示数据质量不一导致的模仿学习扩展难题，该文提出WARP-RM方法，通过时间扭曲增强（变速与倒放）自监督学习帧级相对进度信号，无需人工标注。模型预测帧间归一化时间差，生成密集的正负进度估计，用于WARP-BC中加权高优势动作片段，提升策略鲁棒性。在物理机器人折叠T恤和瓶入桶任务中，即使训练数据质量下降，WARP-BC仍保持19/20成功率，远超普通BC的2/20，吞吐量提升最高达18倍。
Ruby: Unmasking Unsafe Rust in Stripped Binaries via Machine Learning
Authors: Xiang Cheng, Sangdon Park, HyungSeok Han, Xiaokuan Zhang, Taesoo Kim
First: 2022-10-31T19:32:18+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-10T14:48:57+00:00
Comments: Accepted to DSN 2026
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Rust, as an emerging system programming language, introduces $\texttt{unsafe}$ to allow developers to bypass safety checks during compilation. As a result, memory safety bugs are typically confined to the $\texttt{unsafe}$ regions, which have been the primary focus of Rust bug-finding tools. However, such tools rely on the presence of the $\texttt{unsafe}$ keyword in Rust source code; there are no tools available that can examine Rust binaries to pinpoint $\texttt{unsafe}$ areas. Therefore, we propose $\texttt{Ruby}$, the first tool that unmasks $\texttt{unsafe}$ regions in Rust binaries using machine learning. By capturing the subtle differences in the binary instructions, $\texttt{Ruby}$ can identify 91.75% of the total $\texttt{unsafe}$ regions with a false positive rate of 6.16%, beating SOTA LLM models including GPT-5.2, Claude-4.5 and Gemini-3. We further applied $\texttt{Ruby}$ to guide symbolic execution and fuzzing, showing a speed-up of 57.95% and 21.26%, with five bugs confirmed and patched by Google in Android library fuzzing.
中文标题/摘要
标题：Ruby：通过机器学习在剥离二进制文件中揭示不安全的Rust代码
作为新兴的系统编程语言，Rust引入了$\texttt{unsafe}$机制，允许开发者在编译时绕过安全检查。因此，内存安全漏洞通常局限于$\texttt{unsafe}$区域，这也是Rust漏洞检测工具的主要关注点。然而，现有工具依赖于Rust源代码中的$\texttt{unsafe}$关键字；尚无工具能够分析Rust二进制文件以精确定位$\texttt{unsafe}$区域。为此，我们提出了$\texttt{Ruby}$，这是首个利用机器学习在Rust二进制文件中揭示$\texttt{unsafe}$区域的工具。通过捕捉二进制指令中的细微差异，$\texttt{Ruby}$可识别91.75%的总$\texttt{unsafe}$区域，误报率为6.16%，超越包括GPT-5.2、Claude-4.5和Gemini-3在内的最先进大语言模型。我们进一步将$\texttt{Ruby}$应用于符号执行和模糊测试，分别实现57.95%和21.26%的速度提升，并在Android库模糊测试中发现并由Google修复了五个漏洞。
Summary / 总结
Motivated by the need to detect unsafe Rust code in stripped binaries without source-level annotations, Ruby introduces a machine learning approach that analyzes binary instruction patterns to identify unsafe regions. By training on binary features, Ruby achieves 91.75% precision in locating unsafe code with only 6.16% false positives, outperforming state-of-the-art LLMs like GPT-5.2 and Claude-4.5. When integrated into symbolic execution and fuzzing frameworks, Ruby accelerates bug discovery by 57.95% and 21.26%, leading to the identification and patching of five real bugs in Android libraries by Google.
为在无源码的剥离二进制文件中检测不安全的Rust代码，Ruby提出一种基于机器学习的方法，通过分析二进制指令模式识别不安全区域。与依赖源码中unsafe关键字的现有工具不同，Ruby直接作用于编译后的二进制文件，准确率高达91.75%，误报率仅6.16%，超越当前最先进大语言模型。实验表明，Ruby可使符号执行和模糊测试效率分别提升57.95%和21.26%，并助力Google在Android库中发现并修复了五个漏洞。
ProofCouncil: An LLM Agent for Solving Open Mathematical Problems
Authors: Johannes Schmitt, Tim Gehrunger, Jasper Dekoninck, Gergely Bérczi, Uri Kreitner, Liam Price, David Holmes
First: 2026-07-10T14:46:01+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-10T14:46:01+00:00
Comments: 25 pages, 7 figures. ProofCouncil appears as System A (IMProofBench ProofCouncil) in the official FirstProof second-batch report (arXiv:2606.18119). Code and agent-building library: https://github.com/eth-sri/proof-council
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) have shown increasing promise in solving open problems in mathematics. However, their performance can be further improved through agentic workflows tailored to real-world mathematical practice. To this end, we introduce ProofCouncil, a mathematical agent that is designed to tackle open problems using an author-critic architecture. ProofCouncil served as a submission to the second batch of FirstProof, a challenge consisting of 10 real-world mathematical problems that agents must solve autonomously. Its submissions for 6 of the 10 problems were judged by the referees to be correct up to at most minor revisions, showing the best performance among participating teams. We also evaluate ProofCouncil on 30 open problems collected from mathematical researchers. Among the 21 solutions that received human feedback, 5 were judged completely correct, 2 more were judged promising pending final verification, and a further 8 contained useful partial progress. In this short paper, we describe the development of ProofCouncil and the agent-building library used to create it, which we release as open source to the community.
中文标题/摘要
标题：ProofCouncil：一种用于解决开放数学问题的大型语言模型代理
大型语言模型（LLMs）在解决开放数学问题方面展现出日益增长的潜力。然而，通过针对现实世界数学实践定制的代理工作流，其性能可进一步提升。为此，我们引入ProofCouncil，一种采用作者-批评者架构设计的数学代理，旨在解决开放性问题。ProofCouncil作为FirstProof挑战赛第二批次的参赛作品提交，该挑战赛包含10个现实世界数学问题，要求代理自主求解。其在10个问题中的6个提交被评审员认为正确（或仅需微调），表现优于所有参赛团队。此外，我们还在30个由数学研究人员收集的开放问题上评估ProofCouncil。在21个获得人类反馈的解决方案中，5个被判定完全正确，2个被判定为有前景但需最终验证，另有8个包含有用的部分进展。本文简要介绍ProofCouncil的开发过程及其所使用的代理构建库，该库已开源发布。
Summary / 总结
Motivated by the potential of large language models to solve open mathematical problems, the authors developed ProofCouncil, an agent employing an author-critic architecture to autonomously tackle complex math challenges. In the FirstProof challenge, ProofCouncil submitted solutions for six out of ten problems, which referees deemed correct with at most minor revisions, outperforming other teams. On a separate set of 30 open problems, 5 solutions were judged fully correct, 2 were considered promising, and 8 showed useful partial progress, demonstrating the agent’s capability to generate high-quality mathematical reasoning.
为提升大语言模型在解决开放数学问题中的表现，作者设计了ProofCouncil代理，采用作者-批评者架构自主处理复杂证明。在FirstProof挑战赛中，该代理对十道题中的六道提交了被评审员认定正确的解答（仅需微调），表现优于其他团队。在另30道开放问题上，5个解答被判定完全正确，2个被认为有潜力待最终验证，8个包含有用部分进展，验证了其在真实数学推理中的有效性。
LUMOS: Latent Universal Medical Priors for Segmentation
Authors: Zhuonan Liang, Wei Guo, Jie Gan, Yaxuan Song, Runnan Chen, Hang Chang, Weidong Cai
First: 2026-03-01T13:55:34+00:00 · Latest: 2026-07-10T14:42:18+00:00
Comments: 13 pages, 10 figures, 12 tables
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
General vision foundation models (VFMs) have been primarily developed on natural images, and their utility for medical image segmentation is therefore often considered to depend on costly adaptation or domain-specific fine-tuning. In this paper, we revisit this assumption from a different perspective: rather than requiring VFM segmentors to relearn visual regularities, we investigate whether the low-level visual priors necessary for anatomical delineation already lie dormant within general VFMs. We observe that frozen VFMs, despite lacking medical supervision, encode transferable visual regularities. These properties are not exclusive to natural images but are also fundamental to medical image understanding. Motivated by this observation, we propose Latent Universal Medical PriOrs for Segmentation (LUMOS), a novel framework that amplifies general VFM priors to conventional medical segmentors. LUMOS consists of two key components: (1) Pathfinder that distills visual cues from a frozen vision foundation model, and (2) Inspiror that sparks the conventional medical networks with spatial guidance from distilled visual regularities. In this way, the segmentor is relieved from learning complex visual regularities entirely from limited medical annotations and can instead focus on task-specific anatomical delineation. Across diverse medical datasets and token-based VFMs, LUMOS shows that general VFMs can serve as spatial prior generators when their frozen token spaces preserve patch-level pattern relevance. DINO provides stable matched-backbone gains, while SigLIP exposes VFM-specific sensitivity caused by its different token granularity and representation objective.
中文标题/摘要
标题：LUMOS：用于分割的潜在通用医学先验
通用视觉基础模型（VFMs）主要在自然图像上开发，其在医学图像分割中的实用性通常被认为依赖于昂贵的适配或领域特定的微调。本文从不同角度重新审视这一假设：而非要求VFM分割器重新学习视觉规律，我们探究是否用于解剖结构勾画所必需的低级视觉先验已潜藏于通用VFMs中。我们观察到，即使缺乏医学监督，冻结的VFMs仍编码可迁移的视觉规律，这些规律不仅适用于自然图像，也是医学图像理解的基础。受此启发，我们提出Latent Universal Medical PriOrs for Segmentation（LUMOS），一种新框架，旨在将通用VFM先验增强至传统医学分割器。LUMOS包含两个关键组件：（1）Pathfinder，从冻结的视觉基础模型中蒸馏视觉线索；（2）Inspiror，利用蒸馏的视觉规律为空间引导传统医学网络。这样，分割器无需从有限的医学标注中完全学习复杂的视觉规律，而可专注于任务特定的解剖结构勾画。在多样化的医学数据集和基于token的VFMs上，LUMOS表明，当冻结的token空间保留块级模式相关性时，通用VFMs可作为空间先验生成器。DINO提供稳定的匹配骨干增益，而SigLIP则因token粒度和表示目标不同，暴露了VFM特有的敏感性。
Summary / 总结
Motivated by the observation that frozen vision foundation models (VFMs) pretrained on natural images encode transferable visual priors useful for anatomical segmentation, the authors propose LUMOS, a framework that leverages these latent priors without requiring medical-specific fine-tuning. LUMOS consists of two components: Pathfinder, which distills visual cues from frozen VFMs, and Inspiror, which injects these cues as spatial guidance into conventional medical segmentors. Experiments across multiple medical datasets and token-based VFMs show that LUMOS significantly improves segmentation performance by enabling segmentors to rely on pre-learned visual regularities rather than relearning them from limited medical data. Notably, DINO consistently outperforms other models, while SigLIP’s performance varies due to its distinct token granularity and representation learning objective.
受冻结的视觉基础模型（VFMs）在自然图像上预训练后仍能编码可迁移的视觉先验、对医学图像分割有益的启发，作者提出LUMOS框架，无需医学特定微调即可利用这些潜在先验。LUMOS包含两个模块：Pathfinder从冻结VFMs中蒸馏视觉线索，Inspiror将这些线索作为空间引导注入传统医学分割器。在多个医学数据集和基于token的VFMs上实验表明，LUMOS显著提升分割性能，使分割器无需从有限医学标注中重新学习复杂视觉规律，而可专注于任务相关的解剖结构勾画。值得注意的是，DINO在匹配主干结构下表现稳定，而SigLIP则因token粒度与表征目标不同显示出敏感性，表明VFM选择影响效果。
